# Mobility / CARFAX in IHS Markit and S&P Global transcripts

## Coverage notes

- Wrong-event payloads (Quartr served a different transcript; excluded, no entries): 64126 "AGM 2022" (actually a Sustainable1 ESG webinar); 609213 "Fireside chat" 2026-03-18 (actually a Market Intelligence AI-strategy webinar).
- IHS Markit: Quartr holds transcripts only for the Q2 FY2021 (2021-06-23) and Q3 FY2021 (2021-09-28) calls. FY2016–FY2020 and Q4 FY2021 earnings calls are listed without transcripts — not obtainable from this source.
- Event 620148 ("Investor Day 2026", listed under S&P Global) is Mobility Global's own inaugural investor day; it duplicates the MBGL quote bank (transcripts/MBGL_2026-05-12_investor-day.md). Consecutive same-speaker paragraphs were grouped into one entry.
- Scan method: batches read every paragraph, except eight events (24584, 20622, 65410, 165844, 223553, 393534, 673513 and part of 24584) where transcripts were too large to return inline; there mentions were located by keyword search (Mobility, CARFAX, automotive, Polk, automotiveMastermind, Market Scan, dealer, spin) plus surrounding Q&A context. Explicit mentions are captured; an indirect reference without any keyword ("the division") could be missed.
- Borderline entries (portfolio-review or divestiture remarks where Mobility is not named; passing "transportation" remarks pre-merger) are included and flagged as borderline inside the entry — drop them for a strict count.
- Quotes were checked verbatim against transcript text (programmatically where the transcript was saved locally, otherwise by eye). Transcript errors are flagged, not corrected (e.g., "$80 billion-$90 billion" deferred-tax figure, likely millions).

# SPGI - M&A Announcement (S&P Global / IHS Markit merger) - 2020-11-30

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: M&A Announcement
- Date: 2020-11-30
- Event type: m_and_a
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/64130/overview?utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=referral
- Participants: Chip Merritt (SVP IR, S&P Global); Doug Peterson (President and CEO, S&P Global); Lance Uggla (Founder, Chairman and CEO, IHS Markit); Ewout Steenbergen (EVP and CFO, S&P Global); analysts Kevin McVeigh (Credit Suisse), Manav Patnaik (Barclays), Hamzah Mazari (Jefferies), Alex Kramm (UBS), Gary Bisbee (BofA), Judah Sokol (JPMorgan), Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley), Jeff Silber (BMO), Owen Lau (Oppenheimer), Andrew Jeffrey (Truist), Ashish Sabadra (Deutsche Bank). Jonathan Gear and Eric Boyer of IHS Markit were noted as attending.
- Transcript read in full (2 pages, 112 paragraphs scanned). Payload company/title/date matched.
- Mention count: 5 (this is the IHS Markit merger call; there is no CARFAX or "Mobility" unit discussion by name beyond one comment, and no automotive-specific financials)

## Event summary
- The call announces the all-stock S&P Global / IHS Markit merger; the IHS Markit Transportation segment (which housed the automotive/Mobility business) is mentioned only as one of the pillars of the combined company. No segment numbers, no CARFAX, Polk or aM are named.
- Peterson lists "transportation and engineering" among the core market segments of the pro forma company, and Uggla lists "transportation" among IHS Markit's focus segments.
- The only substantive commentary is Peterson's answer to an analyst asking about shedding non-core assets: he calls Transportation "very powerful," high growth, institutional, and "really more of a mobility business," and sees opportunity to push its data into Market Intelligence. He explicitly did not signal a divestiture.
- In the closing Q&A, Peterson names "automotive" data among sets to be moved quickly into the Market Intelligence platform after close.
- Deal context: 0.2838 S&P shares per IHS Markit share (about 67.75/32.25 ownership), about $480M cost and $350M revenue synergies, 6.5%-8% pro forma organic growth.

## Entries

### 1. Prepared - Doug Peterson, President and CEO, S&P Global - [Transportation segment in combined company]
- Quote: "The pro forma company will serve as a global customer base across financial information services, ratings, indices, commodities and energy, and transportation and engineering."
- Detail: Describing the key features of the combined company, Peterson lists the customer-facing segments of the pro forma business: financial information services, ratings, indices, commodities and energy, and transportation and engineering. He says the combination offers differentiated data, analytics, research and benchmarks used in workflows of many leading companies, augmented by Kensho and the IHS Markit Data Lake. No Transportation-specific numbers are given here. Shortly after he cites 76% recurring revenue "balanced across numerous important industry segments" and about $680M of EBITDA synergies.
- Tags: other, M&A
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3475550&documentType=transcript&eventId=64130&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=579.05

### 2. Prepared - Lance Uggla, Founder and CEO, IHS Markit - [IHS Markit overview, includes Transportation]
- Quote: "We're a preeminent provider of deep financial market and industry intelligence across the industry focus segments of financial services, transportation, resources, consolidated markets, and solutions."
- Detail: Uggla gives an overview of IHS Markit: headquartered in London, over 50,000 business and government customers in 140 countries, including 80% of the Fortune Global 500. Transportation is one of the five focus segments (with financial services, resources, consolidated markets and solutions). He stresses deep expertise in competitive global markets and blue-chip client relationships. No Transportation-specific financials. Following paragraphs say IHS Markit expects 6%-8% organic revenue growth in 2021 and pro forma revenue of $11.6B, 76% recurring.
- Tags: other, M&A
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3475550&documentType=transcript&eventId=64130&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=764.71

### 3. Prepared - Doug Peterson, President and CEO, S&P Global - [Transportation engineering among core segments]
- Quote: "The transaction creates businesses with increased scale, world-class products in core market segments, financial information and services, ratings, indices, commodities and energy, and transportation engineering."
- Detail: Peterson restates the strategic rationale: increased scale and world-class products in core segments, including transportation (engineering), with complementary portfolios serving new and expanded use cases and geographies. He adds the merger balances the earnings mix across industry segments, producing a resilient portfolio with more financial flexibility. No Transportation-specific data.
- Tags: other, M&A
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3475550&documentType=transcript&eventId=64130&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=1028.01

### 4. Q&A - Doug Peterson, President and CEO, S&P Global - Alex Kramm (UBS) asks whether the combined company could shed non-core assets and become leaner again given S&P's divestiture history.
- Quote: "It's an institutional business. It's actually really more of a mobility business when you think about what the future is of transportation."
- Detail: Peterson says all IHS Markit businesses fit: indices with S&P indices, Financial Services with Market Intelligence, Energy and Resources with Platts, and some benefit to Ratings. Anticipating that the question may target Transportation, he calls it a very powerful part of the combination, high growth and institutional, and says it is really more of a mobility business given where transportation is heading. It fits well with data and analytics already inside the business, and he sees opportunity to leverage some of that content in the Market Intelligence platform. He ties it to energy transition: EVs affect the whole energy chain (metals used in batteries, battery technology), and S&P can bring learning there, helped by the 451 Research acquisition. He did not propose any divestiture; he emphasized strong fit across all verticals. Uggla adds (next turn) only about the AWS data lake, Kensho and data science, nothing Transportation-specific.
- Tags: other, M&A, drivers, B2B/aM/Polk
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3475550&documentType=transcript&eventId=64130&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=2209.53

### 5. Q&A - Doug Peterson, President and CEO, S&P Global - Ashish Sabadra (Deutsche Bank) asks how cloud lowers the risk of merging large data assets and how the Market Intelligence platform will roll out for IHS Markit product sets.
- Quote: "We think that we will be able to quickly move information like OTC market pricing and data, upstream oil and gas, automotive, private market data, supply chain and trade data, etc., into the Market Intelligence platform, as well as linking our marketplace in the Data Lake."
- Detail: Preceding turns: no platforms need merging, everything is additive; Market Intelligence has 350,000 active users and is close to final rollout; both companies began data-center-to-cloud plans about three years ago with mostly the same vendors, making a cloud-to-cloud integration easier. In this turn, Peterson lists automotive among the IHS Markit data sets (with OTC pricing, upstream oil and gas, private market data, supply chain and trade) that can quickly be moved onto the Market Intelligence platform and linked with the marketplace and Data Lake; work starts immediately and he says they will move as fast as possible at close. No automotive-specific metrics.
- Tags: AI/product, B2B/aM/Polk, M&A
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3475550&documentType=transcript&eventId=64130&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=3597.31

# IHS Markit (INFO) - Q2 2021 (FY Nov) earnings call

- Company: IHS Markit (INFO)
- Event title: Q2 2021 (FY Nov)
- Date: 2021-06-23
- Event type: Earnings call
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/6412?companyId=6412&documentId=3764405&documentType=transcript&eventId=8567&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp
- Participants: Lance Uggla (Chairman and CEO), Jonathan Gear (CFO), Edouard Tavernier (head of Transportation division), Adam Kansler (head of Financial Services), Brian Lawson (head of Resources); analysts from Credit Suisse, Bank of America, Baird, JPMorgan, Jefferies, Stifel, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, BMO, William Blair, Truist, Huber Research, Barclays.
- Mention count: 21 (IHS Markit segment is "Transportation"; CARFAX and automotiveMastermind sit within it. No "Mobility" or "Polk" term used in this call.)

## Event summary
- Transportation organic growth was 39% in Q2 2021 (38% recurring, 41% non-recurring), flattered by the Q2 2020 COVID comparison (pricing concessions, depressed auto trading); EBITDA $171M at 49.6% margin, up 870 bps FX-adjusted.
- Dealer businesses (CARFAX and automotiveMastermind) described as growing rapidly amid tight new and used inventory and soaring used-car prices; CARFAX consumer and dealer transactions drove non-recurring strength.
- FY2021 Transportation organic growth guidance raised to 14-16% from 13-15%; management frames the underlying run-rate as high single digits, not reliant on used or new sales alone.
- Margin gains are partly a "false economy" from temporary 2020 cost cuts (including CARFAX marketing pullback) reversing; margins expected to moderate.
- Connected-car data: POCs at CARFAX and for OEM dealer-network work; real leverage 2-3 years away.
- S&P Global merger expected to close calendar Q4 2021; Automotive/Transportation seen as having no major overlap with S&P, led by Tavernier.

## Entries

### 1
- Speaker / section: Lance Uggla, Chairman and CEO; Prepared
- Quote:
> "Moving on to transportation, which had organic revenue growth of 39% in Q2."
- Detail: Transitions from Financial Services (9% organic) to the Transportation segment, headline organic growth 39% in the quarter.
- Tags: results

# IHS Markit (INFO) - Q3 2021 (FY Nov) earnings call

- Company: IHS Markit (INFO)

- Date: 2021-09-28
- Event type: Earnings call

- Participants: Lance Uggla (Chairman and CEO), Jonathan Gear (EVP and CFO), Adam Kansler (President), Edouard Tavernier (SVP and Head of Transportation), Brian Crotty (EVP), Eric Boyer (Head of IR); analysts Kevin McVeigh (Credit Suisse), Gary Bisbee (BofA), Jeff Meuler (Baird), Alex Kramm (UBS), Hamza Mazari (Jefferies), George Tong (Goldman Sachs), Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley)
- Paragraphs scanned: 52 (single page, full transcript)
- Mention count: 11

## Event summary
- Transportation segment (CARFAX, automotiveMastermind, Polk-type automotive insights, Maritime and Trade) grew 15% organically in Q3, helped by easy COVID-era comps; recurring +17%, non-recurring +8%. FY outlook 14%-16% organic, with underlying growth in the high single digits.
- Transportation adjusted EBITDA margin was 48.1%, down 280 bps FX adjusted, as 2020 benefited from COVID cost actions.
- CARFAX for Life is described as an established dealer service-loyalty product; CARFAX consumer and dealer transactions were strong.
- Supply-chain/chip shortage is a temporary headwind to new-car OEM/dealer marketing products (automotiveMastermind bookings, digital marketing); used-car and service markets are neutral to positive. Management says it is not material and does not change the outlook.
- Management credits the automotiveMastermind acquisition and enterprise rollout (two major dealers) as a key forward component, and cites automotive as supporting the company at the top end of the 5%-7% long-term range for 2022.
- Context: the call was the last as IHS Markit ahead of the S&P Global merger; no separation or spin discussion.

- Detail: Transportation segment posted 15% organic revenue growth in Q3 2021. (Follow-on detail is in the next paragraph.) Part of the segment-by-segment review after financial services (+8%).

# S&P Global (SPGI) - Status Update (merger close call)

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Status Update
- Date: 2022-03-01
- Event type: status_update (update_and_briefing)
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/16001/overview?utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=referral
- Participants: Mark Grant (IR); Doug Peterson (President and CEO); Ewout Steenbergen (EVP and CFO); analysts Manav Patnaik (Barclays), Kevin McVeigh (Credit Suisse), Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley), Alex Kramm (UBS), Hamzah Mazari (Jefferies), Andrew Steinerman (JP Morgan), Ashish Sabadra (RBC), Jeff Silber (BMO), Shlomo Rosenbaum (Stifel), Craig Huber (Huber Research), Owen Lau (Oppenheimer), Jeff Meuler (Baird), George Tong (Goldman Sachs), Andrew Nicholas (William Blair)
- Paragraphs scanned: 99 on page 1 plus 70 on page 2 (169 total) (full transcript read; payload company/title/date confirmed)
- Mention count: 6

## Event summary

- Call held the day after the S&P Global / IHS Markit merger closed. IHS Markit's Transportation division is renamed Mobility and becomes one of six reporting divisions (Market Intelligence, Ratings, Commodity Insights, Mobility, Indices, Engineering Solutions).
- 2022 Mobility guidance: high-single-digit revenue growth with margins in the low 40s percent.
- Drivers: dealer interest in used-car and service offerings; OEM/manufacturing demand for forecast products amid supply-chain uncertainty.
- Mobility is cited as a cross-division revenue-synergy source (expertise into ESG/energy transition) and a Mobility tile (global vehicle registrations, 150+ countries) was added to the S&P marketplace on day one.
- A Mobility/EV information asset is cited as an input to the combined ESG and energy-transition product suite.
- No CARFAX, Polk or automotiveMastermind names are used on this call.

## Entries

### 1. Division naming
- Speaker/section: Doug Peterson, President and CEO, S&P Global; Prepared
- Quote: "IHS Markit's Transportation division will be reported as the Mobility division going forward."
- Detail: Peterson describes the post-merger reporting structure: IHS Markit's Transportation division becomes the Mobility division, while Indices combines relevant products of both companies and IHS Markit's Engineering and Product Design business (inside the Consolidated Markets and Solutions division) becomes Engineering Solutions. The aim is to preserve each franchise's strength while combining capabilities. - Mobility revenue grew 10% year over year in Q1 2022 (first full quarter-set post-merger), led by planning solutions and used car offerings; one of three divisions with double-digit growth (with Commodity Insights and Indices).
- Segment margin: adjusted operating profit up 8%, margin down 80 bps y/y, as expenses rose 11% on advertising and headcount rebuild; TTM margin up about 400 bps to 39%.
- Dealer revenue +12%, retention above pre-COVID levels; manufacturing (OEM) +3% constrained by supply chain; Financials and Other +12% on insurance underwriting.

## Event summary
- Mobility was a clear bright spot in Q3 2022: revenue +8% y/y, adjusted operating profit +14%, margin +200 bps, TTM adjusted margin 40%, 78% recurring revenue.
- Growth was driven by CARFAX new business and high retention; dealer revenue +10% on strong CARFAX demand because dealership profitability stayed high. Marketing products powered by Mobility remained muted.
- Management explained an "inherent hedge" in the business: used-car/CARFAX strength offsets weaker sales and marketing (OEM/dealer) products if the car market normalises (new-car inventories rise).
- Peterson named three trends watched in automotive: used-car market, OEM and new vehicle supply chain recovering from historic lows, and the EV transition. All seen as favourable to Mobility data and analytics.
- Management said Mobility is a "phenomenal business" with strong innovation and launches, and they expect continued good performance. No spin or separation discussion; the context was the 8-month-old IHS Markit merger.

# S&P Global (SPGI) - 2022 Investor Day

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Investor Day 2022
- Date: 2022-12-01
- Event type: Investor Day
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3779438&documentType=transcript&eventId=44944&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp
- Participants: Mark Grant (Head of IR), Douglas Peterson (President and CEO), Ewout Steenbergen (EVP and CFO), Adam Kansler (President, Market Intelligence), Martina Cheung (President, Ratings), Saugata Saha (President, Commodity Insights), Edouard Tavernier (President, S&P Global Mobility), Dan Draper (CEO, S&P Dow Jones Indices), Swamy Kocherlakota (CIO), Yaacov Mutnikas (CTO, Market Intelligence), Bhavesh Dayalji (CEO, Kensho); analysts from RBC, Stifel, Oppenheimer, Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, UBS, William Blair, JPMorgan, BMO, Deutsche Bank, others.
- Mention count: 26 (paragraphs scanned: 324 across four pages, read through the closing remarks)

## Event summary
- Mobility is presented as a $1.4 billion business led by Edouard Tavernier, with a medium-term target of 7-9% organic growth (he is "highly confident" of high single digits) and consistent margin accretion; the range reflects the law of large numbers and long-term investing, with an analyst noting pre-COVID growth was nearer 10%.
- Four growth vectors: planning/forecasting for electrification and the connected car (67 million forecast lines vs a few hundred thousand), digital retail (Polk audiences, automotiveMastermind OEM incentive optimization, third OEM in Q2 2023), used-car (CARFAX listings, Car Care app 28M consumers to 100M+ in 5 years), and S&P cross-leverage (Sustainable1, buy-side/Market Intelligence).
- Business is North America focused, international under 10%; plan to build CARFAX in Europe and a global automotive supply chain data asset; 45,000 dealer relationships with most buying only a fraction of the offering.
- Used car market viewed as resilient; model not tied to transaction counts.
- Cross-division integration: Mobility supply chain data is the 200th Marketplace tile; emissions/transportation data going to financial customers; sustainable mobility team set up with Ratings.
- No spin or separation language; Mobility is treated as a core, integral division.

# S&P Global (SPGI) - Q4 2022 earnings call

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Q4 2022
- Date: 2023-02-09
- Event type: Earnings call
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3733338&documentType=transcript&eventId=40414 (event 40414)
- Participants: Doug Peterson (President and CEO), Ewout Steenbergen (EVP and CFO), Adam Kansler (President, Market Intelligence), Martina Cheung (President, Ratings), Mark Grant (SVP IR); analysts incl. George Tong (Goldman), Alex Hess (JPM), Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley), Alex Kramm (UBS), Jeff Silber (BMO), Craig Huber, Faiza Alwy (Deutsche Bank), Owen Lau (Oppenheimer), Stephanie Moore (Jefferies), Brendan (for Manav Patnaik, Barclays), Jeff Meuler (Baird), Ashish Sabadra (RBC), Russell Quelch (Redburn), Adam Wang (for Shlomo Rosenbaum, Stifel)
- Paragraphs scanned: 168 (two pages)
- Mention count: 7

## Event summary
- Mobility (reported as a division) grew revenue 9% in Q4 2022, led by Dealer (+9%, CARFAX subscriptions), Manufacturing (+8%, Polk Automotive Solutions and recall deals) and Financials and other (+10%, insurance underwriting). Expenses rose 15% (headcount, ad timing, cloud), cutting operating profit 2% and margin by 380 bps; TTM margin 39%.
- 2023 guidance for Mobility: revenue +6.5% to +8.5%, margin 39%-40%, driven by auto supply-chain normalization, price realization, new business and new product adoption; expense growth expected to moderate quickly and be below revenue growth.
- On Q&A, management said Mobility is resilient across used, new and EV markets: OEM/dealer margin pressure from normalization could hit retention, offset by higher demand for marketing and sales products; confident in the 6.5%-8.5% range.
- CARFAX, automotiveMastermind and Polk are named as the product set serving OEMs, suppliers, dealers, insurers and lenders.
- Mobility's e-fees revenue is included in the new "sustainability and energy transition" revenue base ($247M in 2022 versus $209M under old ESG definition).
- A portfolio-review comment (Engineering Solutions sale to KKR already announced) was made in a Mobility-related question; no Mobility separation was announced.

### 2. Ewout Steenbergen, EVP and CFO - Prepared
- Quote: "Dealer revenue increased 9% year-over-year, driven by strong demand for CARFAX subscription products."

## Event summary
- Mobility revenue grew 10% YoY in Q1 2023 (dealer +10%, manufacturing +11%, financials and other +11%), with adjusted operating profit +14% and margin +130 bps. CARFAX new business, recall activity and planning solutions drove growth; Market Scan added about 1 point (about $3-4M).
- Offsetting the strength, Mobility retention declined modestly as the market normalizes from used-car-heavy conditions toward new cars, mainly on the dealer side where margins are under pressure.
- Full-year: Mobility revenue guidance raised (helped by Market Scan, expected to add about 150 bps of Mobility growth) while Mobility margin guidance was tuned down because Market Scan is modestly dilutive.
- Management framed the outlook as positive: demand for forecasting data, sales and marketing products among dealers and OEMs, EV-related planning products, CARFAX product launches, and data flowing to insurers, banks and lenders. Mobility also posted double-digit Vitality revenue.
- Global vehicle sales growth cited: US about 7.5%, China about 6%, Asia (incl. Japan/Korea) about 6.5%, Europe about 7.5%.

- Quote: "As expected and previously discussed, retention rates in Mobility have declined modestly as automotive inventory levels and volumes start to normalize."
- Detail: In the customer section, Peterson notes that retention was strong and stable company-wide, improving in Commodity Insights and some other products, but declining modestly in Mobility as automotive inventory and volumes normalize (this was expected and previously flagged). He also notes some lengthening of sales cycles in parts of the business, mainly financial services customers focused on margin protection. He says that in meetings with customers, including large automotive OEMs, retailers, asset managers and banks, the common theme is that they want to do more with S&P Global, and there is a large opportunity to raise awareness of the product breadth.

Manufacturing +4% on elevated recall activity and marketing solutions; Financials and other +9%.

# S&P Global (SPGI) - 2023 Goldman Sachs Financial Services Conference

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)

## Event summary
- The moderator framed Mobility as the strongest growth segment, guided up 9-10% for full-year 2023, driven by CARFAX, CARFAX for Life and automotiveMastermind.
- Steenbergen sees no reason for the high growth to change: a once-in-a-century industry shift to EVs and hybrids drives demand for Mobility planning/forecasting solutions.
- Normalizing retention (inventory rising) is offset by OEMs and dealers spending more on marketing, which benefits automotiveMastermind, the marketing engine behind many dealers and OEMs.
- Business described as well run, highly entrepreneurial with a steady cadence of new product launches; outlook positive for the next phase.
- No spin or separation commentary; portfolio-simplification remark at the end was general (not Mobility specific).

## Entries

### 1
- Speaker / section: Ewout Steenbergen, EVP and CFO; Q&A. Moderator (Goldman Sachs) asks how sustainable current growth is within Mobility, the strongest segment, guided up 9-10% for the year and driven by CARFAX, CARFAX for Life and automotiveMastermind.
- Quote:
> "It's one of the high growth businesses in the portfolio, and there's no reason, from my perspective, to believe that that will change-"
- Detail: First reason is structural: the biggest revolution in the industry in about a century as traditional carmakers move to EVs and hybrids and must rethink production lineups, supply chains, factory locations and battery sourcing. They need data and research support, which Mobility's planning solutions provide, and industry upheaval is generally good for S&P. Framing figures (from the question): 9-10% full-year growth guide.
- Tags: results, guidance, drivers, AI/product

# S&P Global (SPGI) - Stifel 2024 Cross Sector Insight Conference

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Stifel 2024 Cross Sector Insight Conference
- Date: 2024-06-04
- Event type: Investor conference (fireside chat)
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2700773&documentType=transcript&eventId=199854 (event 199854)
- Participants: Doug Peterson (CEO, S&P Global); Shlomo Rosenbaum (Managing Director, business services analyst, Stifel, host)
- Paragraphs scanned: 68 (single page, full transcript)
- Mention count: 4

## Event summary
- The only Mobility segment of the chat was a direct question from the host on Mobility's growth runway and fit within S&P. No financial numbers (growth rate, margin, guidance) were given for Mobility.
- Peterson frames Mobility as a data and analytics business serving an industry undergoing a massive shift (ICE to electric, hybrid, potentially hydrogen) and serving OEMs, dealers, recall management, CARFAX used-car information, and insurers/lenders.
- Runway argument: the business kept growing through the pandemic chip shortage because the used-car shift increased demand for its data; he sees ample room to grow as long as the industry is transforming and people keep buying cars.
- The MarketScan acquisition adds vehicle pricing and market-value data, which he says completes the supply-chain data set and is difficult for any competitor to match (Rosenbaum names Experian in the UK).
- On fit: Mobility benefits from S&P technology, AI and data-extraction tools; Peterson notes some end customers differ and says the company is "always evaluating the total portfolio, especially at the product level", but calls Mobility a really good business, with no separation or spin announced.

## Entries

### 1. Doug Peterson, CEO - Q&A
- Analyst question (Shlomo Rosenbaum, Stifel): Mobility was IHS Markit's fastest-growing business; how long can the growth last and how does it fit within S&P?
- Quote: "And it's exciting to be in a data and analytics business when you're serving an industry that is shifting from ICE, internal combustion engine, systems to electric, potentially hydrogen, to hybrid."
- Detail: Automotive and mobility globally are going through a massive transformation, creating doubt and uncertainty across the supply chain down to consumers; S&P supplies data and analytics for informed decisions. Products serve manufacturers; dealers and dealer groups working with OEMs on inventory management, rebates and interest-rate subsidies; and recall management for the industry. CARFAX provides individual used-car information (value arises "one second after it leaves" the lot) used for buying and selling, plus aggregate statistics. A less-discussed client set is insurers and financial institutions using the data for underwriting and lending. He describes a very broad customer set and an industry that has been growing quickly, and says Mobility has benefited from S&P Global technology, AI and data-extraction tools.
- Tags: drivers, CARFAX, B2B/aM/Polk, AI/product
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2700773&documentType=transcript&eventId=199854&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=1309.38

### 2. Doug Peterson, CEO - Q&A
- Analyst question: same (Rosenbaum), continued answer on fit with S&P.
- Quote: "The customers, especially some of them, are not necessarily the same end customers, but we're always evaluating the total portfolio, especially kind of at the product level, what makes sense and what fits, but this is a really good business, and one that's, for us, it's a strong business to keep learning about in the portfolio."
- Detail: Peterson acknowledges Mobility's customers are not necessarily the same end customers as the rest of S&P, says the company continually evaluates the total portfolio at the product level for fit, but characterizes Mobility as a strong business that S&P is still learning about. No decision or plan on separation is stated.
- Tags: spin, M&A, other
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2700773&documentType=transcript&eventId=199854&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=1420.8

### 3. Doug Peterson, CEO - Q&A
- Analyst question (Rosenbaum): is there enough runway for Mobility's growth to continue?
- Quote: "The used car market completely took off."
- Detail: During the pandemic, chip and parts shortages made vehicle manufacturing a struggle; the used-car market took off, and the Mobility business kept growing because it could supply information to manufacturers, lenders, insurers and dealers for that shift, and "benefited tremendously". He concludes that as long as the industry is transforming and people keep buying cars, there is a lot of room to grow.
- Tags: drivers, risk, CARFAX
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2700773&documentType=transcript&eventId=199854&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=1454.88

### 4. Doug Peterson, CEO - Q&A
- Analyst question: same (Rosenbaum), continued; Peterson adds the MarketScan acquisition.
- Quote: "We've made an acquisition in the business of a company called Market Scan, and this is a specialized business for the automotive sector that has the best information about value of cars, pricing and value of cars."
- Detail: Before MarketScan, Mobility had data on which cars would be in demand, what to build and when buyers would buy, but not what they would pay or market value. MarketScan fills that gap, giving "a very complete set of information and data" across the supply chain. Peterson calls it a unique, valuable asset that is hard to recreate.

Operating profit +10%, margin +60 bps to 40.9%; TTM margin down 10 bps to 38.8%.
- Subscription revenue is about 81% of Mobility revenue and grew 10.5%; recall is described as high margin but non-recurring and abnormally soft, which is the main drag.
- Guidance: Mobility revenue growth range tightened and slightly lowered to 8%-9% (from 8.5%-10%), and margin guidance trimmed 50 bps to 38.5%-39.5%, both attributed to expected continued softness in recall.

# S&P Global (SPGI) - Q1 2025 earnings call (Mobility spin announcement)

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Q1 2025
- Date: 2025-04-29
- Event type: q_1 (earnings_call)
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/314678/overview?utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=referral
- Participants: Mark Grant (VP of Investor Relations); Martina Cheung (President and CEO); Eric Aboaf (CFO); analysts Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley), Faiza Alwy (Deutsche Bank), Surinder Thind (Jefferies), Ashish Sabadra (RBC), Andrew Steinerman (JP Morgan), Scott Wurtzel (Wolfe), Alex Cram (UBS), Manav Patnaik (Barclays), George Tong (Goldman Sachs), Tom Ross (William Blair), Craig Huber (Huber Research), Owen Lau (Oppenheimer), Jeff Silber (BMO), Shlomo Rosenbaum (Stifel), Russell Quelch (Redburn Atlantic), Jason Haas (Wells Fargo), Jeff Meuler (Baird), Sean Kennedy (Mizuho), Joshua Dennerlein (BofA)
- Paragraphs scanned: 88 on page 1 plus 37 on page 2 (125 total) (full transcript, both pages read; company/title/date confirmed via event record)
- Mention count: 17

## Event summary

- S&P Global announced its intent to spin Mobility into a standalone public company: tax-free, expected to complete in 12-18 months, subject to market conditions and customary regulatory approvals; Investor Day set for Nov 13 in New York with a refreshed multi-year strategy for the four core divisions.
- Mobility scale: 2024 revenue of $1.6 billion at an adjusted operating margin of nearly 40%, versus nearly $13 billion revenue and about 50% margin for the four core divisions. More than 70% of Mobility revenue is tied to the used car market and more than 80% is subscription.
- Q1 2025 Mobility results: revenue +9% (about 70 bps FX headwind), dealer +11% (CARFAX, automotiveMastermind), manufacturing +1% (recall-transaction decline, lapping from Q2), financials and other +11%; segment margin 38.5% (+40 bps). Full-year Mobility revenue guidance unchanged.
- CARFAX: Car Care has about 46 million users (nearly +65% since the 2022 investor day), backed by more than 35 billion vehicle history records.
- Tariffs: management expects some budget pressure with OEM clients but says subscription structure and the used-car mix insulate the unit; the used market may benefit from higher new-car prices.
- Rationale and carve-out: management framed the spin as best path to long-term shareholder value and greater focus; initial stranded/dis-synergy costs look immaterial to S&P Global; more detail on capital structure and licensing later in 2025-26.

- Speaker/section: Martina Cheung, President and CEO; Q&A (Craig Huber, Huber Research, asked why spin now when Mobility was previously called core and the stock has tracked the S&P 500)
- Quote: "To be clear, the Mobility business is a phenomenal business."
- Detail: Cheung (after an audio drop she answers from the top) says this has been considered for some time with deep analysis. Mobility has a long history of innovation, strong growth and talented leaders. - Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3781865&documentType=transcript&eventId=314678&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=3288.9

### 16. Auto tariffs sensitivity
- Speaker/section: Martina Cheung, President and CEO; Q&A (Jason Haas, Wells Fargo, asked about Mobility's sensitivity to auto tariffs, any impact so far, customer conversations)
- Quote: "However, recall that this is largely on a subscription basis, and there we are reasonably insulated from those impacts."
- Detail: Management expects tariff-driven reduced imports and potentially lower manufacturing (imported components), which could pressure existing inventories and raise prices, and anticipates possible budget pressure in dialogue with OEM manufacturing clients. The OEM business is largely subscription so reasonably insulated, and S&P is helping manufacturers navigate. The business is about 70% used-car related, and the used market could benefit from higher new-car prices and lower new inventory. Vehicle history, pricing and incentive/sales and marketing data are critical to dealers in this environment. Some pressure on end customers but no direct impact yet, which is a reason for confidence in the full-year guide (3883s continuation).

# SPGI - Bernstein 41st Annual Strategic Decisions Conference
- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Bernstein 41st Annual Strategic Decisions Conference
- Date: 2025-05-29
- Event type: conference fireside chat
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/347558/overview?utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=referral
- Participants: Martina Cheung (President and CEO, S&P Global); Moderator (Bernstein, unnamed in transcript)
- Mention count: 5 (66 paragraphs scanned)

## Event summary
- Cheung explains the rationale for the announced Mobility spin: a deep vertical SaaS business serving the whole auto value chain (suppliers, OEMs, dealers, consumers), about 70% tied to the used car market, with dealers the largest customer group; it cannot be cross-sold S&P products, so it is best as a standalone company.
- Mobility financial profile cited: 8.4% growth last year and 39% margin.
- Disynergies are expected to be very limited and minimal; data shared with other S&P units can be handled via arm's-length agreements.
- Timing: the spin will take about 12-18 months; it is intended to be a 100% tax-free spin; more disclosures are promised through the year.
- The standalone company would have more freedom to invest and pursue organic and inorganic growth. Capital return guideline stays at 85%, and OSTTRA proceeds (about $1.4B) go to buybacks.

## Entries

### 1. Moderator (Bernstein) - Q&A opening question
- Question: Moderator opens by asking how Cheung's view of the company has evolved as CEO, referencing the Mobility spinoff announcement and policy/macro volatility.
- Quote: "You've announced the spinoff of the Mobility Division."
- Detail: The only Mobility reference is the moderator's framing, listing the spinoff announcement among the early events of Cheung's tenure. Cheung's answer (customer focus, Chief Client Office, unified data organization for generative AI) does not discuss Mobility.
- Tags: spin
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2542982&documentType=transcript&eventId=347558&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=31.74

### 2. Martina Cheung, President and CEO - Q&A (two turns)
- Question: Why spin Mobility off now, and what is the strategic rationale?
- Quote: "The Mobility business we view as a deep vertical SaaS-based business serves the entire automotive value chain all the way from suppliers to OEM to dealerships and to the end consumer. About 70% of the business is directly related to the used car market."
- Detail: She has thought about Mobility for some time and personally loves the business, citing a leadership team that has grown it compellingly: 8.4% growth last year and a 39% margin, with consistently high innovation. The rationale rests on the end customer: a vertical SaaS business across the automotive value chain, about 70% used-car related, where dealerships are the largest customer revenue base and also the largest number of customers. S&P would not go to a dealer to sell the other S&P products, so unlocking enterprise value from Mobility is hard. Synergies existed and were delivered, but the business is best served as a standalone company with strong products, leadership, customer base and growth/margin profile and multiple growth paths.
- Tags: spin, results, margin/costs, B2B/aM/Polk, drivers
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2542982&documentType=transcript&eventId=347558&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=430.98 (continuation https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=2542982&documentType=transcript&eventId=347558&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=488.22)

### 3. Martina Cheung, President and CEO - Q&A (two turns)
- Question: What is the impact of the Mobility spin on rest of S&P: synergies, shared data platforms, margin profiles?
- Quote: "We have said that we think disynergies would be very limited, very minimal. Practically speaking, it's going to take about 12- 18 months."
- Detail: Disynergies are expected to be very limited. Parts of Mobility already operate independently and are less integrated than something like Ratings. Data that Mobility provides to other S&P groups can be continued through arm's-length agreements during the spin process. The separation takes about 12-18 months and the team is moving fast through the first phase. More disclosures will be shared with investors over the course of the year.
- Tags: spin, margin/costs, risk

### 4. Martina Cheung, President and CEO - Q&A (two turns)
- Question: What constraints has Mobility had under S&P governance and what operational advantages would it have standalone?
- Quote: "It is a group of leaders who can turn investment dollars very quickly around opportunity and runway for additional growth. I do think that when you look at that business and think again about the composition of the end customers, their ability to grow both organically and different pathways, whether it is organic, inorganic, et cetera, we think will be much better served as a standalone business."
- Detail: Mobility is highly innovative and good at converting investments into fast-growing products. Recent investments are only beginning to show benefits. # SPGI - Investor Day 2026 (Mobility Global Inaugural Investor Day) - 2026-05-12

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI); the event is Mobility Global's inaugural investor day ahead of its planned spin-off from S&P Global
- Event title: Investor Day 2026
- Date: 2026-05-12
- Event type: investor_day
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/620148/overview?utm_source=mcp&utm_medium=referral
- Participants: Tejal Engman (Head of IR, Mobility Global); Bill Eager (President of S&P Global Mobility and CEO, Mobility Global); Joe Lafeir (President, Mobility Business Solutions, the B2B segment); Scott Fredericks (President, CARFAX); Jeremy Morehouse (Head of AI Office); Matt Calderone (CFO); Q&A analysts Jeff Meuler (Baird), Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley), Alex Kramm (UBS), Manav Patnaik (Barclays), Jason Haas (Wells Fargo)
- Payload company/title/date matched (S&P Global event ID 620148; content is Mobility Global's Investor Day, 2026-05-12). Transcript read in full (3 pages, 258 paragraphs).
- Mention count: 90 (the entire event is about Mobility; consecutive paragraphs by the same speaker on the same theme are grouped into one entry, so entry count is lower than paragraph count)
- Note: the transcript renders some numbers loosely (e.g., "$1.5 billion vehicles" for 1.5 billion vehicles; "$80 billion-$90 billion" deferred tax cash impact likely means millions; "$32/$3,200" dealers for automotive Mastermind; "$150 million" consumers target means 150 million).

## Event summary
- Mobility Global (S&P Global's Mobility division: CARFAX plus the B2B Polk/automotiveMastermind/planning businesses) plans to complete its spin from S&P Global to become an independent public company in mid-2026. The company has filed a Form 10, announced a board and leadership team, secured a $500M revolver, expects to issue about $2B of bonds (mostly dividended to S&P) and to launch with $150M+ of cash, targeting investment grade and gross leverage of 2.5x or below.
- Scale and mix: revenue of about $1.75B (2025 growth 8.5%), about 65% CARFAX and 35% B2B (over $600M, 82% subscription; CARFAX over 80% subscription). Over 90% of revenue is driven by proprietary data. 40,000+ CARFAX dealers, 53M consumers in the Car Care app (goal 150M US/Canada, 1B global), 40 of 40 top OEMs, 94 of top 100 suppliers; 3,400 employees.
- Guidance and targets: 2026 organic growth guide reaffirmed at 7.5%-9%; medium-term (2028+) organic growth 7.5%-10% (core 5%-7%, mostly value), adjusted EBITDA growth 8%-11%, margin about 40% historically with a brief reset from $20M-$25M of standalone costs, 3%-4% equity compensation and $75M-$100M one-time costs, then 50 bps+ expansion; return 75%+ of FCF (dividend payout 20%-25% of net income; buybacks not before 2027); medium-term tax rate 25%-27%; NRR meaningfully above 100%.
- Strategy and products: integrating five legacy businesses into one; cross-sell between B2B (automotiveMastermind in about 3,200 dealers vs CARFAX 40,000) and CARFAX; CARFAX Lifetime Program (1,600 dealers, +19 pts service retention); Behavior Prediction Score, FAST (90% workflow automation, 4x forecast quality), Data Studio/EEQ (40% close-rate improvement); AI (99.5% model accuracy, agentic/MCP, Car Fox video product with 25M views per month); international (Germany for CARFAX in 2026, China OEM expansion, India acquisition).
- Risks and caveats: recall revenue is the one weak line (non-recurring); affordability and 84-month loans; standalone cost reset; competition framed as fragmented by lifecycle stage.

She introduces the Car Fox mascot before a video.

- Quote: "We got started in the information space in 1870. 1870 when R. L. Polk started this business."

### 60. Prepared - Matt Calderone, CFO, Mobility Global - [acyclicality and COVID]
- Quote: "we are not just counter-cyclical, we're relatively acyclical."

(1) $20M-$25M incremental run-rate public-company expense, reaching run rate in about 8 to 12 months, while the transition services agreement (TSA) with S&P ramps down; S&P has been a "phenomenal" partner. (2) Equity compensation increases (next entry). (3) One-time costs (later entry).

Return of 75%+ of free cash flow via a dividend payout ratio of 20%-25% of net income (to maintain the dividend aristocrat status inherited from S&P) and share repurchases, which likely will not begin until 2027. ### 75. Q&A - Bill Eager, CEO - Jeff Meuler (Baird) asks how Mobility competes on the CARFAX side versus Experian AutoCheck and on the Polk side versus GlobalData/LMC Automotive.
- Quote: "If you think about it, we don't compete with anyone directly across that entire thing."
- Detail: Eager frames competition by stage of the two lifecycles: each stop (shop, buy, service, sell; or planning and sales) has different competitors. Joe Lafeir adds on planning: there is a fundamental difference in level of detail, and competitors tend to offer low-cost, very different products; the company has branched into additional products off the forecasting data. On the sales side, competitors operate at different points of the life cycle, including the core legacy Polk market reporting; the company has wrapped more assets (inventory, pricing data) around the core data asset, moving into adjacencies and knitting together a full life cycle competitors have not.
- Tags: B2B/aM/Polk, CARFAX, risk
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3346612&documentType=transcript&eventId=620148&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=9298.41

### 76. Q&A - Scott Fredericks, President, CARFAX - (same question as above, CARFAX vs AutoCheck)
- Quote: "The cost of the CARFAX report as a percent of that continues to decline."
- Detail: The Vehicle History Report is driven by consumer wants: the most data, wrapped in a brand they trust. For a dealer, the CARFAX report is a relatively low-cost part of a rising total car price; the dealer would risk losing the consumer's confidence by substituting another brand when the consumer says "show me the CARFAX." Most consumers do not pay; the dealer typically provides the report free online and in the showroom, though some consumers buy directly online.

### 77. Q&A - Bill Eager, CEO - Toni Kaplan (Morgan Stanley) asks whether data-provider relationships are long-term or exclusive.
- Quote: "Shops have a massive problem, and that is that every year they lose 22%, 23% of the vehicles that they work on, right?"
- Detail: Eager says the shop relationships began about 20 years ago and the value returned has grown with the asset; with 177,000 sources, agreements are a mix (not all exclusive) and the value exchange creates the relationship. Shops lose track of vehicles when ownership changes; CARFAX is the connecting tissue: shop service history appears on the report, the buyer is more likely to return to that shop. Fredericks adds examples: shops get data back and brand exposure on the report; more than 6,000 police agencies get investigative tools in exchange for their data.

### 78. Q&A - Bill Eager, CEO - Jeff Meuler (Baird) asks in what ways the data is superior to AutoCheck and what is unique in Polk, Market Scan and automotive Mastermind data.
- Quote: "We'll put it under a code name."
- Detail: Eager says the culture around data is "paranoid": some sources are known by only about three people and are kept under code names (a source might be touched by 500 people who do not know which it is), and the company will not publish the list of 177 sources. Over decades they learned to accept data in any format (even paper) and to detect feed changes; relationships were built with OEMs, dealers, municipalities and shops over 30-40 years.

### 81. Q&A - Matt Calderone, CFO - Alex Kramm (UBS) asks about pricing and retention by business and where pricing power is unused.

Pricing is not a simple P x Q: they are not a software company, with many solutions and customers; the approach is adding value then capturing it, not a fixed 3%-5% annual increase. ### 1. Cheung, Q&A (Christian Bolu: Mobility and the spin, said to go live July 1; what is RemainCo's identity beyond the financial cleanup?)

# SPGI - Mizuho Technology Conference 2026
- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Mizuho Technology Conference 2026

- Participants: Mark Grant (SVP Investor Relations and Treasurer, S&P Global); Sean Kennedy (Analyst, Mizuho)
- Mention count: 2 (97 paragraphs scanned)

## Event summary
- The Mobility spin is expected to complete on July 1 (2026), and the capital-structure side is the only Mobility topic covered.
- $2 billion of debt was raised at Mobility Global and dividended up to S&P Global; use of proceeds is share repurchases plus some debt reduction, with the spin intended to be leverage neutral for S&P.
- Capital return target for 2026 is raised from at least 85% of free cash flow to 100%, implying roughly $4.5 billion of buybacks this year.
- Grant says appetite for tuck-in M&A has diminished and there is no appetite for anything transformational.
- No operating detail on Mobility or CARFAX.

## Entries

### 1. Sean Kennedy, Analyst, Mizuho - Q&A (question)
- Question: Given the Mobility spin effective July 1 and $2B debt placed at Mobility Global, how should investors think about capital strategy: buybacks, tuck-in M&A, or deleveraging?
- Quote: "I guess the larger kind of strategic action that you've been taking and planning for the last year or so was the mobility spin, effective July 1st, and the $2 billion of debt placed at Mobility Global."
- Detail: The analyst frames the Mobility spin (effective July 1) and the $2B of debt at Mobility Global as the key strategic action of the past year, and asks whether capital allocation would tilt to buybacks at current share price levels, more tuck-in M&A (unique datasets), or deleveraging.
- Tags: spin, M&A
- Link: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977?companyId=3977&documentId=3545309&documentType=transcript&eventId=687477&navigation=external&utm_medium=referral&utm_source=mcp&targetTime=2178.33

### 2. Mark Grant, SVP Investor Relations and Treasurer - Q&A (answer, two turns)
- Question: Same capital strategy question as above.
- Quote: "With the completion of the mobility spin, which we expect to take place on July 1st, you're right, we've raised $2 billion of debt financing that gets dividended out to S&P Global. We want to make sure that the spin's leverage neutral for us."
- Detail: The $2B of debt financing raised by Mobility Global is dividended to S&P Global; stated use of proceeds is share repurchases and some debt reduction, and the spin is meant to be leverage neutral. The public capital-return target is at least 85% of free cash flow annually via dividends and buybacks; with the Mobility proceeds it is raised to 100% in 2026. That works out to roughly $4.5 billion of repurchases this year, the main focus given valuation and the high return on buying back stock. That raises the bar for tuck-in M&A; they have said there is no appetite for anything transformational and the appetite for tuck-ins has diminished somewhat.

# S&P Global (SPGI) - Q2 2026 earnings call

- Company: S&P Global (SPGI)
- Event title: Q2 2026
- Date: 2026-07-28
- Event type: Earnings call (q_2)
- Quartr event URL: https://web.quartr.com/companies/3977/events/656135/overview
- Participants: Martina Cheung (President and CEO), Eric Aboaf (EVP and CFO), Mark Grant (SVP Investor Relations and Treasurer); analysts including Faiza Alwy, Andrew Steinerman, David Motemaden, Jeff Meuler, Christian Bolu, Jason Haas, Sean Kennedy, Owen Lau
- Paragraphs scanned: 124
- Mention count: 10

## Event summary

- First call after the Mobility spin: Mobility Global completed spinning off on July 1, 2026; Mobility moves to discontinued operations from Q3, so FY guidance (GAAP and adjusted) now excludes Mobility; a July 6, 2026 release provided recast financials ex-Mobility (four quarters of 2025, FY2025 and Q1 2026).
- S&P now has four divisions: Ratings, Indices, Energy, Market Intelligence (451 Research and maritime and trade moved from MI to Energy).
- Excluding Mobility lowers overall revenue growth slightly (Mobility historically higher growth but modestly lower margins) and lifts margins; Ratings and Indices outperformance offsets, so consolidated organic constant currency growth stays 6%-8%; consolidated margin ex-OSTTRA to expand 75-100 bps.
- S&P received about $2 billion dividend from Mobility Global at completion; buybacks raised to over $7 billion for 2026; first-half FCF of $2.4B included Mobility; H2 ex-Mobility FCF guided to $2.9-3.1B.
- No Mobility operating results or CARFAX/dealer commentary on the call.

- Detail: Recast financials excluding Mobility were provided for the 4 quarters of 2025, FY2025, and Q1 2026, available at investor.spglobal.com.
